Title: Das Gesäng der einsamen und verlassenen Turtel-Taube, nemlich der christlichen Kirche
Creator: The Ephrata community; about two-thirds of the 277 hymns by Conrad Beissel, who also wrote the preface on singing
Publisher: Ephrata, Pa.: Drucks der Brüderschafft
Publication Date: 1747
Rights: Public Domain (published 1747, more than 95 years ago; public domain by date under the project's rolling rule; the item carries no rights field, so the date rule alone applies)
Language: deu
Source: Internet Archive item dasgesngdereinsa00ephr (https://archive.org/details/dasgesngdereinsa00ephr), the item's own dasgesngdereinsa00ephr_djvu.txt full-text OCR derivative; contributing library per the item metadata: Princeton Theological Seminary Library; host metadata date: 1747. Fetched directly (curl/urllib, raw bytes) by the Library thread (Era VIII pass), 2026-10-16. sha256 of the raw fetched bytes: a0f98a5fa4a044b9f8c7776d8cd7c789106e29cce2b7f40f2a76ee94607702d8.
Provenance note: Header prepended by the Library thread; nothing else altered, no OCR error corrected, nothing retyped. The first ninety lines are scan noise and a garbled title page, left in place because no clean marker separates them from the text (the OCR reads the title as "Turtel Sonde"). Body hymn text is usable Fraktur OCR.
Content note: The central Ephrata hymnal. Beissel's preface is his treatise on music; its English (1903) is vendored in sachse_music-of-ephrata-cloister-beissel-treatise_1903.txt. No PD English of the hymns as a whole exists.

those familiar with the subject. Many copies stop at page 294, 
others at page 419. This copy, not only has the whole 495 pages, but 
a number of additional hymns inserted. The book is exceedingly 
scaree.’—[S. W. P., 1882.]
